Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s changed, I guess. Now it’s like I know I should want to go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to show up. I’ve even been bad about answering texts from people, which I know isn’t right. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… about four months now. It sort of started after my wife went back to working full-time, and I’ve been home by myself most weekdays. I kept thinking it would pass once I got used to the routine, but it’s… it’s still been there. Does that sound right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest, and it’s hard to shake. Some days I end up just sitting out in the garage and crying, and I can’t even tell you what set it off. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s changed, I guess. I used to really look forward to choir, playing a little pickup ball, working on cars… even grilling for everybody. Now it’s like I know I should want to go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to show up. I’ve even been bad about answering texts from people, which I know isn’t right. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… about four months now. It sort of started after my wife went back to working full-time, and I’ve been home by myself most weekdays. I kept thinking it would pass once I got used to the routine, but it’s… it’s still been there. Does that sound right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want you to think I’m trying to get attention or anything. But yeah, the thought keeps coming up that I should just be dead. And when I’m by myself, it scares me, because I start thinking I might actually do something to make that happen. Is that… okay to say that here?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how quickly or slowly you're moving compared to how you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if it’s noticeable to other people, but it feels like everything I do is slower. Like my body’s moving through mud or something. Even when someone asks me a question, I hear it, I understand it, but it takes me a while to get the words to come out. I’m not trying to be difficult… it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed my clothes fitting much differently or anything. I haven’t been keeping track of my weight, though… should I have been?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious exactly. I feel uneasy sometimes, like I can’t quite settle, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would hurt yourself if you were to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want you to think I’m trying to do something right now. I wish I could say yes. I mean, I used to have things… choir, playing pickup ball, working on cars, grilling when the family was around. Those were things I looked forward to.

And resting doesn’t really seem to give me anything back, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but yes. I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest, and it’s hard to shake. Some days I end up just sitting out in the garage and crying, and I can’t even tell you what set it off. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s changed, I guess. I used to really look forward to choir, playing a little pickup ball, working on cars… even grilling for everybody. Now it’s like I know I should want to go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to show up. I’ve even been bad about answering texts from people, which I know isn’t right. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… about four months now. It sort of started after my wife went back to working full-time, and I’ve been home by myself most weekdays. I kept thinking it would pass once I got used to the routine, but it’s… it’s still been there. Does that sound right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want you to think I’m trying to get attention or anything. But yeah, the thought keeps coming up that I should just be dead. And when I’m by myself, it scares me, because I start thinking I might actually do something to make that happen. Is that… okay to say that here?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how quickly or slowly you're moving compared to how you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if it’s noticeable to other people, but it feels like everything I do is slower. Like my body’s moving through mud or something. Even when someone asks me a question, I hear it, I understand it, but it takes me a while to get the words to come out. I’m not trying to be difficult… it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed my clothes fitting much differently or anything. I haven’t been keeping track of my weight, though… should I have been?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious exactly. I feel uneasy sometimes, like I can’t quite settle, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would hurt yourself if you were to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want you to think I’m trying to do something right now. But the thought keeps coming up that maybe I should just be dead. And when I’m alone, it scares me, because I start wondering if I might actually do something to make that happen. I’m sorry… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about a specific method to hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have one clear method picked out, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that the thought keeps coming back that I should just be dead, and when I’m alone I get scared because I start thinking I might actually do something to make that happen. I’m sorry, I know that’s not a very good answer.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or that you're looking forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I used to have things… choir, playing pickup ball, working on cars, grilling when the family was around. Those were things I looked forward to.\n\nLately, though, I just… I can’t seem to make myself care enough to go. People text me about it and I don’t even answer sometimes. I know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. It’s just like the interest isn’t there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days it’s like I just can’t feel much enjoyment from anything.\n\nI used to really look forward to choir, pickup ball, working on cars, grilling for the family… and now I can’t seem to make myself care enough to show up. Sometimes people text me about it and I don’t even answer. I know that’s not good. It’s just… the pleasure part feels gone most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are interfering with your ability to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not working right now, so I don’t want to overstate it like I’m missing shifts or anything. But at home, yes. I’m not keeping up with the house the way I should.\n\nI had all these handyman plans, little projects I was supposed to do, and I just… don’t follow through. Things sit there, and my wife ends up worrying about everything.\n\nAnd I guess the part I’m embarrassed to say is she’s scared to leave me alone sometimes, because when the thoughts get really bad, I don’t really trust myself either. I know that’s probably important to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… nearly every day, I’d say. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest most mornings.\n\nSome days I just end up sitting out in the garage and crying, and I can’t even tell you what set it off. It’s embarrassing to admit, but… yeah, it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are worse in the morning or are they pretty consistent throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s probably worse in the morning, I think. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest, and it kind of sets the tone.\n\nSometimes it eases a little later, but some days I just end up sitting out in the garage crying and I don’t even really know what started it. I’m sorry… I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But yeah, the thought keeps coming up that I should just be dead. And when I’m by myself, it scares me, because I start thinking I might actually do something to make that happen. Is that… okay to say that here?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how quickly or slowly you're moving compared to how you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if it’s noticeable to other people, but it feels like everything I do is slower. Like my body’s moving through mud or something. Even when someone asks me a question, I hear it, I understand it, but it takes me a while to get the words to come out. I’m not trying to be difficult… it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed my clothes fitting much differently or anything. I haven’t been keeping track of my weight, though… should I have been?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious exactly. I feel uneasy sometimes, like I can’t quite settle, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would hurt yourself if you were to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want you to think I’m trying to do something right now. But the thought keeps coming up that maybe I should just be dead. And when I’m alone, it scares me, because I start wondering if I might actually do something to make that happen. I’m sorry… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about a specific method to hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have one clear method picked out, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that the thought keeps coming back that I should just be dead, and when I’m alone I get scared because I start thinking I might actually do something to make that happen. I’m sorry, I know that’s not a very good answer.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or that you're looking forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I used to have things… choir, playing pickup ball, working on cars, grilling when the family was around. Those were things I looked forward to.\n\nLately, though, I just… I can’t seem to make myself care enough to go. People text me about it and I don’t even answer sometimes. I know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. It’s just like the interest isn’t there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days it’s like I just can’t feel much enjoyment from anything.\n\nI used to really look forward to choir, pickup ball, working on cars, grilling for the family… and now I can’t seem to make myself care enough to show up. Sometimes people text me about it and I don’t even answer. I know that’s not good. It’s just… the pleasure part feels gone most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are interfering with your ability to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not working right now, so I don’t want to overstate it like I’m missing shifts or anything. But at home, yes. I’m not keeping up with the house the way I should.\n\nI had all these handyman plans, little projects I was supposed to do, and I just… don’t follow through. Things sit there, and my wife ends up worrying about everything.\n\nAnd I guess the part I’m embarrassed to say is she’s scared to leave me alone sometimes, because when the thoughts get really bad, I don’t really trust myself either. I know that’s probably important to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… nearly every day, I’d say. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest most mornings.\n\nSome days I just end up sitting out in the garage and crying, and I can’t even tell you what set it off. It’s embarrassing to admit, but… yeah, it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are worse in the morning or are they pretty consistent throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s probably worse in the morning, I think. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest, and it kind of sets the tone.\n\nSometimes it eases a little later, but some days I just end up sitting out in the garage crying and I don’t even really know what started it. I’m sorry… I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to find some moments of happiness or relief from your sad feelings, even if they're brief?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe here and there, but it doesn’t last. I used to look forward to things, you know—choir, playing a little pickup ball, working on cars, grilling for everybody.\n\nNow I just… I can’t seem to make myself care enough to go. People text me and I’ll see it, and I just don’t answer. It sounds bad saying it out loud, but it’s like those things should make me feel better, and they just don’t. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would hurt yourself if you were to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want you to think I’m trying to do something right now. But the thought keeps coming up that maybe I should just be dead. And when I’m alone, it scares me, because I start wondering if I might actually do something to make that happen. I’m sorry… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about a specific method to hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have one clear method picked out, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that the thought keeps coming back that I should just be dead, and when I’m alone I get scared because I start thinking I might actually do something to make that happen. I’m sorry, I know that’s not a very good answer.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or that you're looking forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I used to have things… choir, playing pickup ball, working on cars, grilling when the family was around. Those were things I looked forward to.\n\nLately, though, I just… I can’t seem to make myself care enough to go. People text me about it and I don’t even answer sometimes. I know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. It’s just like the interest isn’t there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days it’s like I just can’t feel much enjoyment from anything.\n\nI used to really look forward to choir, pickup ball, working on cars, grilling for the family… and now I can’t seem to make myself care enough to show up. Sometimes people text me about it and I don’t even answer. I know that’s not good. It’s just… the pleasure part feels gone most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are interfering with your ability to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not working right now, so I don’t want to overstate it like I’m missing shifts or anything. But at home, yes. I’m not keeping up with the house the way I should.\n\nI had all these handyman plans, little projects I was supposed to do, and I just… don’t follow through. Things sit there, and my wife ends up worrying about everything.\n\nAnd I guess the part I’m embarrassed to say is she’s scared to leave me alone sometimes, because when the thoughts get really bad, I don’t really trust myself either. I know that’s probably important to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… nearly every day, I’d say. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest most mornings.\n\nSome days I just end up sitting out in the garage and crying, and I can’t even tell you what set it off. It’s embarrassing to admit, but… yeah, it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are worse in the morning or are they pretty consistent throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s probably worse in the morning, I think. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I wake up with this heavy, hopeless feeling in my chest, and it kind of sets the tone.\n\nSometimes it eases a little later, but some days I just end up sitting out in the garage crying and I don’t even really know what started it. I’m sorry… I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to find some moments of happiness or relief from your sad feelings, even if they're brief?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe here and there, but it doesn’t last. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything significant that I’ve noticed, anyway. My clothes don’t seem, you know, dramatically looser or anything.\n\nI haven’t really been keeping track on a scale, though, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. Is that okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
